Let's design a robust multi-start gradient/multi-step algorithm:
1. Choose main length N = len(height_sequence_1) if available and reasonable (e.g., 100..20000). If unavailable, N=4806.
2. Generate a pool of initial sequences of length N:
   - c sequence (central binomial) normalized.
   - reversed c (increasing).
   - c symmetrized (c_i + c_{n-1-i}) maybe.
   - height_sequence_1 if valid score finite and maybe good; normalize if nonneg.
   - random Dirichlet positive.
   - c * exp(ε noise) with small ε.
   - filtered c with random Q (short) if possible.
   Evaluate each with internal or exact, choose top few.
3. For each of top starts, run iterative local optimization with time allocation. Use exact evaluate_sequence periodically (since evaluator is authoritative; internal FFT may have error and clamping). But exact O(n^2) direct; internal FFT faster. Use internal for acceptance many steps; exact for best candidates every maybe 100 steps or when internal improves by threshold.
4. Optimization submethods:
   a. Smooth p-norm mirror descent (with adaptive p and learning rate) on normalized a.
   b. Block multiplicative perturbations: choose random intervals, multiply by exp(δ*shape) (maybe low-pass), normalize, accept if internal score improves (hill climbing) or with Metropolis occasional. This can escape c. Use different scales.
   c. Coordinate random restarts: zero-out small entries? Maybe.

FFT helpers:
```
def next_power_of_two(x):
    p=1
    while p < x: p*=2
    return p
```

```
def fft_convolve_self(a):
    n=a.size
    need = 2*n-1
    L=1
    while L < need: L <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:need]
    # due roundoff negative tiny
    # no need max(0) for score? use b
    return b
```
For gradient conv, need Lgrad=nextpow2(3n-2). We can compute A once:
```
def fft_score_and_weights(a, beta):
    n=a.size
    m=2*n-1
    need_grad=3*n-2
    L=1
    while L<need_grad: L*=2
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    b_full=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:m]
    # maybe b_full small negative => set 0? score max maybe.
    b=np.maximum(b_full,0)
    S=np.sum(a)
    score=2*n*max(b)/S**2
    # weights stable
    # if beta large: logw = beta*(log(b+eps) - maxlog)
    maxb=np.max(b)
    # use b directly softmax with beta; if maxb==0? 
    logb=np.log(b+1e-300)
    # To avoid overflow, z = beta*(logb - np.max(logb)); w=np.exp(z); w/=sum
```

    x=x-np.max(x)
    e=np.exp(np.clip(x, -50, 50))

Adam optimizer:
```
def adam_step(x, m, v, t, grad, lr, b1, b2):
   m = b1*m + (1-b1)*grad
   v = b2*v + (1-b2)*grad*grad
   m_hat=m/(1-b1**t)
   v_hat=v/(1-b2**t)
   x = x - lr*m_hat/(np.sqrt(v_hat)+1e-8)
   x = x - np.mean(x)
   x = np.clip(x, -50, 50)
   return x,m,v
```
But if all x clipped at -50, zeros approximate. Fine.

Let's include a "filter search":
```
def filter_sequence_n(c, p1, p2, r1, r2):
   a = c.copy()
   if p1>0: a[p1:] += r1*c[:-p1]
   if p2>0: a[p2:] += r2*c[:-p2]
   if min(a) < -1e-8: return None
   a = np.clip(a,0,None); normalize; return
```
Search p1,p2 from ranges, r ranges. Evaluate many (e.g., 2000) using internal score (FFT for each maybe too slow: 2000*FFT ~20s okay). Keep best. Then run optimizer from best filtered.

We need final code not too enormous but thorough. Let's outline code:
```
import numpy as np
import time
import math
# helpers: soft score, grad, c sequence, make candidates, local search
```
No scipy necessary maybe. Use numpy only to reduce dependency and avoid optimizer complexity. But scipy allowed; we can use only numpy.

Global access `height_sequence_1` may not exist? It will. Need handle if NameError? We can try except NameError.

Detailed helper functions:
1. `clean_sequence(seq)`: convert to np array float, clip [0,1000], handle NaN. If invalid, None.

Local block proposal function:
```
def block_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
   n=a.size
   width = int(rng.integers(max(1,n//1000), max(2,n//20)))
   start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
   if rng.random()<0.5: delta = np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,width))
   else:
      t=np.linspace(0,1,width)
      delta=np.exp(sigma*(rng.normal(0,0.5,width) + rng.normal(0,0.5)*np.sin(2*np.pi*t)))
   c=a.copy(); c[start:start+width]*=delta
   return normalize(c) or a
```

Use `np.maximum(b,0,b)` to avoid negatives. `np.max(b)` may be slightly off if small; okay. For weights, if maxb<=0 (all zeros) uniform. Use `z = q * (np.log(b) - maxlog)` for positive. For b zeros, log = -inf; set z=-1e6. Implementation:
```
def weights_power(b, q):
    m = b.size
    maxb = float(np.max(b))
    if maxb <= 0: return np.full(m, 1.0/m)
    thresh = maxb * 1e-300
    logb = np.empty(m)
    pos = b > thresh
    logb[pos] = np.log(b[pos])
    logb[~pos] = -1e6
    z = q * (logb - logb[pos].max())
    # for max z=0; for very small may underflow to 0
    w = np.exp(z)
    sw = w.sum()
    if not finite or sw==0: uniform
    return w/sw
```
But computing max of logb each time okay.
Maybe simpler `z = q * (b - maxb) / max(1, maxb)`? Not scale-free? Since maxb ~1/n, using q*(b-maxb)/maxb equivalent to q log? For ratios, use b/maxb log. We'll use log.

import numpy as np
import time, math

def _norm_arr(seq): ...
def _softmax_vec(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    x = x - x.max()
    # clip exp to avoid under/overflow
    x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 50.0)
    e = np.exp(x)
    s=e.sum()
    if s <=0 or not finite: e=np.ones_like(x)/x.size
    return e/s
```

`central_binom`:
```
def central_binom(n):
    c=np.empty(n); c[0]=1.0
    for i in range(n-1): c[i+1]=c[i]*(2*i+1)/(2*i+2)
    return c
```
For n large, loop 20000 okay. Could use math.prod? Loop.

`fft_score(a, L)`:
```
def fft_score(a, L):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=a.size
    m=2*n-1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, n=L)
    b=b[:m]
    # numerical negatives
    if b.size:
       np.maximum(b,0.0,b)
       maxb=float(np.max(b))
    else: maxb=0.0
    S=float(np.sum(a))
    if S<=0: return np.inf, None
    return 2.0*n*maxb/(S*S), b
```
If want avoid returning b to save, for score only maybe not store? We need b sometimes. For many score, returning b memory. We can have `fft_score_only` without b? But needs b to max. Can't avoid. Return b maybe discarded; memory okay.

`weights_power(b, q)` as above. To avoid storing logb for all, could sort top K? But fine.

`compute_grad(a,q,L)`:
```
def compute_grad(a,q,L):
    n=a.size; m=2*n-1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,n=L)
    b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,n=L)[:m]
    np.maximum(b,0,b)
    S=a.sum(); score=2*n*max(b)/S*S
    w=weights_power(b,q)
    wb=float(np.dot(w,b))
    wf=np.zeros(L,float)
    wf[:m]=w[::-1]
    fw=np.fft.rfft(wf,n=L)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(fa*fw,n=L)
    C=conv[m-1 : m-n-1 : -1].copy()
    grad=2.0*a*(C - wb)
    grad -= grad.mean()
    return grad, score
```
Check `m-n-1` = n-2. If n=1, m=1, slice 0: -1? For n=1 edge. We can handle n=1 separately, but N>=2. For n=2, m=3, stop=0? m-n-1=0, slice 2:0:-1 -> [2,1]? length2, good. Need indices n-1 down to? n=2 indices 2,1. C_i = conv[m-1-i] i=0 idx2, i=1 idx1. Good.

Adam update within propose:
```
def adam_update(x, m, v, grad, t, lr):
   b1=0.9; b2=0.999
   m = b1*m + (1-b1)*grad
   v = b2*v + (1-b2)*grad*grad
   mhat = m/(1-b1**t)
   vhat = v/(1-b2**t)
   x_new = x - lr*mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
   x_new = x_new - x_new.mean()
   x_new = np.clip(x_new, -50, 50)
   return x_new, m, v
```
If t large, 1-b1**t ~1; computing powers each step maybe. Use approximate `bias1=0.9**t` but pow. Fine.
Maybe use simpler momentum: m = b1*m+(1-b1)*grad; x -= lr*mhat? If use normalized Adam, good.

Local search after gradient:
```
def local_block_search(a, L, rng, deadline, best_score):
   a=a.copy(); score=fft_score(a,L)[0]
   sigma=0.3; noimp=0
   while time<deadline:
      width = int(rng.integers(max(1,n//2000), max(2,n//20)))
      start = int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
      cand=a.copy()
      # random lowpass block
      if rng.random()<0.3:
         t=np.linspace(0,1,width,endpoint=False)
         amp=float(rng.normal(0,sigma))
         cand[start:start+width] *= np.exp(amp*np.sin(np.pi*t) + rng.normal(0,sigma*0.5,width))
      elif rng.random()<0.8:
         cand[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,width))
      else:
         # zero/mix
         if rng.random()<0.5: cand[start:start+width]=0
         else: cand[start:start+width]=a[start:start+width]*float(rng.uniform(0,2))
      cand=nan_to_num/clean/norm
      s, _=fft_score(cand,L)
      if s < score:
          a=cand; score=s; sigma=min(1.0,sigma*1.05); noimp=0
      else:
          sigma*=0.95; noimp+=1
          if noimp>200:
             # restart from a plus large noise or best
             a = a + rng.normal(0,0.1,n); a=clean
             sigma=0.5; noimp=0

Filter sample function:
```
def make_filtered_c(c, n, rng):
   a=c.copy()
   r = float(rng.normal(0, 0.1))  # small?
   p = int(rng.integers(1, max(2,n//2)))
   if p < n:
      a[p:] += r*c[:-p]
      if rng.random()<0.5:
         p2=int(rng.integers(p+1,max(p+2,n)))
         r2=float(rng.normal(0,0.1))
         a[p2:]+=r2*c[:-p2]
   if np.min(a) < -1e-8: return None
   a=np.clip(a,0,1e3)
   a=normalize_sum(a,1.0)
   return a
```

```
def _as_valid_array(x):
    try: arr=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64)
    except: return None
    if arr.ndim !=1 or arr.size==0: return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)): arr=np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
    return arr
```

```
def _normalize(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a,0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=1e-300: return np.zeros_like(a)
    return a/s
```

`central_binom` handle n<=0.

`fft_score` with parameter L optionally. If L not multiple? Need L >=2n-1. For state L may be >=3n-2. Good.
```
def fft_score(a,L):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return inf
    m=2*n-1
    if L is None:
       L=1
       while L<m: L <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,n=L)
    b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,n=L)
    b=b[:m]
    # If numerical small negative, max
    np.maximum(b,0.0,out=b)
    maxb=float(b.max()) if b.size else 0.0
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S <=0: return inf,None
    sc=2.0*float(n)*maxb/(S*S)
    return sc,b
```
`b` length m; if L <m, truncation invalid; ensure.

def weights_power(b,q):
    m=b.size
    maxb=float(b.max()) if m else 0.0
    if maxb <=0: return np.full(m,1.0/m)
    # avoid log of zero: use b/maxb then log? 
    rel = b / maxb
    # rel in [0,1]
    # log rel where >0
    z = np.full(m, -1e100)
    pos = rel > 1e-300
    np.log(rel, out=z, where=pos) # careful np.log out with where? It still log zeros? Use temp.
```

def make_filtered_c(c,n,rng):
   a=c.copy()
   # one or two shifts
   for _ in range(2):
      if a.size <=1: break
      p=int(rng.integers(1, n)) # p from1 to n-1
      if p < n:
         r=float(rng.uniform(-0.25, 0.5))
         a[p:] = a[p:] + r*c[:-p]
   if np.min(a) < -1e-8:
      return None
   a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   return _normalize(a)
```

Local block search function:
```
def local_block_search(a, L, rng, deadline):
   a=_normalize(a)
   score,_=fft_score(a,L)
   best=a.copy(); best_score=score
   sigma=0.25; no=0
   n=a.size
   while time.monotonic()<deadline:
      if no>300: # restart
         a=best * np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.2,n)); a=np.clip(a,0,1); a=_normalize(a); no=0; sigma=0.5
      width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//2000), max(2,n//20)))
      start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
      cand=a.copy()
      choice=rng.random()
      if choice <0.5:
         delta=np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,width))
      elif choice <0.8:
         t=np.linspace(0,1,width); delta=np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,1)*np.sin(np.pi*t)+rng.normal(0,sigma*0.5,width))
      else:
         delta=np.ones(width); delta[rng.random_choice? cannot choose element easily? use idx=int(rng.integers(0,width)); delta[idx]=0.0 maybe
      cand[start:start+width]*=delta
      cand=np.clip(cand,0,1000)
      cand=_normalize(cand)
      s,_=fft_score(cand,L)
      if np.isfinite(s) and s < score:
         a=cand; score=s
         if s < best_score:
            best=a.copy(); best_score=s
         sigma=min(1.0, sigma*1.05)
         no=0
      else:
         sigma=max(0.02, sigma*0.95)
         no +=1
   return best,best_score
```

       elif elapsed < 1600: q=32
       else: q=64
       si = it % K
       a=states_a[si]

       if np.isfinite(cur_score) and cur_score < states_score[si]:
          states_score[si]=cur_score
       x_new, m_new, v_new = _adam_update(states_x[si], states_m[si], states_v[si], grad, it+1, lr)
       a_new = _softmax_vec(x_new)
       new_score,_ = fft_score(a_new,L)
       # Optionally small random jitter before score? no.
       improved = np.isfinite(new_score) and new_score < states_score[si]
       accept = improved
       if not accept and np.isfinite(new_score) and np.isfinite(states_score[si]):
          rel = (new_score - states_score[si]) / max(1e-18, states_score[si])
          if rel < 0.05:
             if rng.random() < math.exp(-rel/0.01): accept=True
       if accept:
          states_x[si]=x_new; states_m[si]=m_new; states_v[si]=v_new; states_a[si]=a_new; states_score[si]=new_score

Let's double-check `compute_grad` when q is int but weights_power. If b has all zero? score inf? maybe. If `maxb=0`, weights uniform, C computed, gradient maybe; but a sum zero invalid. Softmax a sum1 so b not zero.

Potential issue in `weights_power`: Using `rel = b / maxb`; if maxb very small (like 1e-300) division okay. If maxb inf? b finite. If b has NaN? fft no NaN unless a NaN. We clean. If `z.max()` nan? handle.
```
def weights_power(b, q):
    m=b.size
    if m==0: return np.empty(0)
    maxb=float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(maxb) or maxb <=0:
        return np.full(m, 1.0/m)
    rel=b/maxb
    z=np.full(m, -1e300)
    pos = rel > 1e-300
    if np.any(pos):
        z[pos] = q * np.log(rel[pos])
        z -= z.max()
    w=np.exp(z)
    sw=float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(sw) or sw <=0:
        return np.full(m,1.0/m)
    w /= sw
    return w
```
Since z for pos max at most 0; after z-=max, zeros -> -1e300 - max? Might underflow but fine. If max becomes negative? z max 0. Good.

`_adam_update`: `bias1 = 1.0 - 0.9 ** float(t)` may underflow for large t? 0.9^t small, bias ~1. Good. Use `pow` maybe. Simpler:
```
bias1 = 1.0 - 0.9**t
bias2 = 1.0 - 0.999**t
```
For t large, pow underflow to 0; okay. t int up to maybe 100k; 0.999**100k ~ e^-100 ~3e-44; fine.
```
if not finite grad: return x, m, v
```

gmax=float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
if gmax > 10.0: grad = grad * (10.0/gmax)
```

Let's implement `reconstruct_from_b(q,n)` with loop and dot.
```
def reconstruct_from_b(q, n):
    q=np.asarray(q,float)
    if q.size<n or q[0] <= 0: return None
    a=np.zeros(n)
    a0=np.sqrt(q[0]); a[0]=a0
    for j in range(1,n):
        if j == 1: R=0.0
        else:
            R=float(np.dot(a[1:j], a[j-1:0:-1]))
        val=q[j]-R
        if val <= 1e-15:
            a[j]=0.0
            # Can still continue? If a_j=0, future R can be computed; q future may be infeasible. We can set q future? Just continue with a[j]=0.
        else:
            a[j]=val/(2*a0)
        if not np.isfinite(a[j]) or a[j] < -1e-9: return None
    a=np.maximum(a,0)
    return a
```
Need also respect max M? If val negative due target impossible, set 0. But q_j may be <R, meaning no nonnegative a_j can achieve q_j; setting 0 makes actual b_j=R>q_j, max maybe >M. We can skip if many infeasible. Check after reconstruct full score, accept if better.

def projection_perturb(a,L,strength):
   b,_=fft_score(a,L)
   n=a.size
   M=float(b[:n].max())
   if M<=0: return None
   q=b[:n].copy()
   # clamp to M
   q=np.minimum(q,M)
   # add noise to q normalized by M
   if strength>0:
       q += strength*M*rng? need rng. Let's pass rng.
   # maybe push towards M for entries below:
   q = 0.7*q + 0.3*M
   q[q<0]=0; q=np.minimum(q,M)
   new_a=reconstruct_from_b(q,n)
   if new_a is None: return None
   new_a=np.maximum(new_a,0)
   s_new = fft_score(new_a,L)[0]
   return new_a,s_new
```

Function `_to_array`:
```
def _to_array(seq):
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if arr.ndim != 1:
        arr = arr.ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return arr
```

`_normalize`:
```
def _normalize(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros_like(a)
    if s > 1000.0: ??? Scale invariant, but values >1000 maybe. If sum>1000, values may >1000 after normalize sum1 so max maybe1; fine. Divide by s.
    a = a/s
    # If any >1000? sum1 no.
    return a
```

`next_pow2`:
```
def next_pow2(x):
    p=1
    while p < int(x): p <<=1
    return p
```

`_adam_update`: Ensure arrays mutable. If grad not finite, return. After x update center and clip.
```
def _adam_update(x,m,v,grad,step,lr):
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
        grad=np.nan_to_num(grad, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    grad_mean = float(np.mean(grad))
    grad = grad - grad_mean
    gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
    if gmax > 10.0:
       grad = grad * (10.0/gmax)
    b1=0.9; b2=0.999
    m = b1*m + (1-b1)*grad
    v = b2*v + (1-b2)*(grad*grad)
    bias1 = 1.0 - 0.9**step
    bias2 = 1.0 - 0.999**step
    mhat = m / bias1
    vhat = v / bias2
    update = mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
    x_new = x - lr*update
    # center to avoid overflow
    x_new = x_new - np.mean(x_new)
    x_new = np.clip(x_new, -50, 50)
    return x_new,m,v
```
If step large, `0.999**step` may underflow to0; bias2=1. If step int >10000, 0.999**10000 ~3.7e-5 not underflow; 100000 ~1e-5; fine.

`make_filtered_c`: Use c raw. If p random. If a min < -1e-5 return None. If all zero return None.
```
def make_filtered_c(c,n,rng):
    a=c.copy()
    tries = int(rng.integers(1,3))
    for _ in range(tries):
        if n <=1: break
        p=int(rng.integers(1,n))
        if p < n:
           r=float(rng.uniform(-0.2,0.4))
           a[p:] += r*c[:-p]
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return None
    if float(np.min(a)) < -1e-5: return None
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <=0: return None
    return a/s
```
Maybe r too small; extend -0.5,0.8? Negative may cause many invalid. Use `rng.uniform(-0.35, 0.7)`. If invalid skip. Good.

`build_initial_candidates`:
```
def build_initial_candidates(c_raw, base_arr, N, L, rng, deadline):
   cands=[]
   c_norm=_normalize(c_raw)
   cands.append(c_norm)
   if base_arr is not None and base_arr.size==N:
      bnorm=_normalize(base_arr)
      if float(bnorm.sum())>0: cands.append(bnorm)
   # variants noise
   for sig in (0.05,0.2,0.6):
      a=c_raw*np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,sig,N))
      a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
      cands.append(_normalize(a))
   rev=_normalize(c_raw[::-1]); cands.append(rev)
   sym=_normalize(c_raw+c_raw[::-1]); cands.append(sym)
   alt=np.ones(N); alt[1::2]=0.5; cands.append(_normalize(c_raw*alt))
   # maybe endpoint emphasis: c_raw * (1 + 0.5*np.arange(N)/N)
   tilt = _normalize(c_raw*(1+0.5*np.linspace(0,1,N)))
   cands.append(tilt)
   scored=[]
   for a in cands:
     if a is None: continue
     s,b=fft_score(a,L)
     if np.isfinite(s): scored.append((s,a))
   count=0
   max_eval=250
   while time.monotonic() < deadline and count < max_eval:
      a=make_filtered_c(c_raw,N,rng)
      if a is not None:
         s,_=fft_score(a,L)
         if np.isfinite(s): scored.append((s,a))
      count +=1
   if not scored: return [c_norm], np.inf
   scores=np.array([s for s,_ in scored], dtype=np.float64)
   order=np.argsort(scores)
   top=[]
   for idx in order[:5]:
      top.append(scored[int(idx)][1])
   return top, float(scores[int(order[0])])
```
Memory scored 250 arrays. Could duplicates cause top arrays all same? Fine.

`reconstruct_from_b`:
```
def reconstruct_from_b(q,n):
   q=np.asarray(q,dtype=np.float64)
   if q.size < n or q[0] <= 1e-300: return None
   a=np.zeros(n)
   a0=float(np.sqrt(q[0]))
   a[0]=a0
   for j in range(1,n):
       if j > 1:
          R=float(np.dot(a[1:j], a[j-1:0:-1]))
       else:
          R=0.0
       val=float(q[j]) - R
       if val <= 1e-15:
          a[j]=0.0
       else:
          aj=val/(2.0*a0)
          if not np.isfinite(aj) or aj < -1e-9:
             return None
          a[j]=aj if aj > 0.0 else 0.0
   a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
   a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
   return a
```
Note if val negative but we set 0, actual b_j=R > q_j; max could increase. We can check after full score. If too many infeasible? Could return None if val < -1e-8 some? We can track `bad` count, if > n//20 return None. But setting 0 may still valid lower score? Add:
```
bad=0
if val <=0:
   if val < -1e-8: bad +=1
   if bad > n//20: return None
   a[j]=0
```

def projection_perturb(a,L,rng,strength):
    n=a.size
    score,b = fft_score(a,L)
    if b is None: return None,np.inf
    # use first n coefficients
    first=b[:n]
    M=float(np.max(first))
    if M <= 0: return None,np.inf
    q=first.copy()
    # Mix with flat M by factor
    mix = float(rng.uniform(0.2,0.7))
    q = (1.0-mix)*q + mix*M
    # Clamp 0..M*(1+strength?) Maybe allow slightly above? Better keep <=M to not increase max? But if >M maybe scale? score unrelated. Let's allow q <= M*(1+0.2)
    q = np.clip(q, 0.0, M*(1.0+0.2*strength))
   noise = float(rng.uniform(0.0,0.2*strength))
    q = q + noise*M*rng.normal(0,1,n)
    q = np.clip(q,0.0, M*(1.0+0.3*strength))
    new_a=reconstruct_from_b(q,n)
    if new_a is None: return None, np.inf
    new_a=_normalize(new_a)
    if new_a is None: return None, inf
    s,_=fft_score(new_a,L)
    return new_a, s
```

def local_block_search(a,L,rng,deadline):
    a=_normalize(a)
    n=a.size
    score,_=fft_score(a,L)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score
    sigma=0.25; no=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       if no > 400:
          a=best*np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.25,n))
          a=np.clip(a,0,1000); a=_normalize(a); score,_=fft_score(a,L); no=0; sigma=0.5
       width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//2000), max(2,n//20)))
       start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
       cand=a.copy()
       choice=float(rng.random())
       if choice < 0.45:
          delta=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,sigma,width))
       elif choice < 0.75:
          t=np.linspace(0.1,0.9,width,endpoint=False)
          amp=float(rng.normal(0.0,sigma))
          delta=np.exp(amp*np.sin(np.pi*t) + rng.normal(0.0,0.5*sigma,width))
       else:
          delta=np.ones(width)
          idx=int(rng.integers(0,width))
          delta[idx]=float(rng.uniform(0.0,1.5))
          if rng.random() < 0.2: delta[idx]=0.0
       cand[start:start+width] *= delta
       cand=np.clip(cand,0,1000)
       cand=_normalize(cand)
       s,_=fft_score(cand,L)
       if np.isfinite(s) and s < score:
           a=cand; score=s
           if s < best_score:
               best=a.copy(); best_score=s
           sigma=min(1.0,sigma*1.05)
           no=0
       else:
           sigma=max(0.02,sigma*0.97)
           no +=1
    return best, best_score
```

```python
"""
Search program for nonnegative step-function heights minimizing
  2*n*max(convolve(a,a)) / sum(a)**2.

The code starts from strong analytic initializers, most notably the
negative binomial / central-binomial family whose square coefficients are
flat on the first half.  It then applies a multi-start mirror/Adam descent
on log-positive heights, periodic filter-perturbations, and a local block
hill-climb.  FFTs are used for fast internal scoring and gradients; the
authoritative evaluate_sequence() is called selectively for validation.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math


def _to_array(seq):
    """Convert input to a 1D finite float array or return None."""
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if arr.ndim != 1:
        arr = arr.ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return arr


def _normalize(a):
    """Normalize nonnegative array to sum 1, staying structurally valid."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros_like(a, dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def next_pow2(x):
    """Smallest power of two >= x."""
    p = 1
    x = int(max(1, x))
    while p < x:
        p <<= 1
    return p


def central_binom(n):
    """
    c_k = binomial(2k,k)/4^k.
    This family has c*c coefficients equal to 1 on the first n-1 positions,
    making it a very strong initial point.
    """
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    c = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    c[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(n - 1):
        c[i + 1] = c[i] * (2.0 * i + 1.0) / (2.0 * i + 2.0)
    return c


def fft_score(a, L):
    """
    Internal FFT-based score.  Returns (score, b) where b is the full
    linear convolution.  This is used for fast search; exact validation
    uses evaluate_sequence().
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf"), None
    m = 2 * n - 1
    if L is None or int(L) < m:
        L = next_pow2(m)
    else:
        L = int(L)

    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, n=L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, n=L)
    b = b[:m]
    np.maximum(b, 0.0, out=b)

    maxb = float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 0.0:
        return float("inf"), b

    score = (2.0 * float(n) * maxb) / (s * s)
    return score, b


def weights_softmax_rel(b, beta):
    """
    Weights proportional to exp(beta*(b/maxb - 1)).
    This is a normalized surrogate max-weighting, robust to scale.
    """
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    m = b.size
    if m == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    maxb = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(maxb) or maxb <= 0.0:
        return np.full(m, 1.0 / m, dtype=np.float64)

    rel = b / maxb
    z = float(beta) * (rel - 1.0)
    np.clip(z, -700.0, 0.0, out=z)
    w = np.exp(z)
    sw = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(sw) or sw <= 0.0:
        return np.full(m, 1.0 / m, dtype=np.float64)
    return w / sw


def compute_grad(a, beta, L):
    """
    Compute a descent direction for log-softmax variables x with a=softmax(x),
    based on weighted square coefficients.

    For a=softmax(x), with weights w over b=conv(a,a), a useful direction is
       grad_x = 2 * a * (C - w·b),
    where C_i = sum_j w_j a_{j-i}.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    m = 2 * n - 1
    L = int(L)

    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, n=L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, n=L)
    b = b[:m]
    np.maximum(b, 0.0, out=b)

    maxb = float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64), float("inf")
    score = (2.0 * float(n) * maxb) / (s * s)

    w = weights_softmax_rel(b, beta)
    wb = float(np.dot(w, b))

    wf = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    wf[:m] = w[::-1]
    fw = np.fft.rfft(wf, n=L)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(fa * fw, n=L)

    # C_i = conv[m-1-i], for i=0..n-1.
    C = conv[m - 1:m - n - 1:-1]

    grad = 2.0 * a * (C - wb)
    grad = np.nan_to_num(grad, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    grad = grad - float(np.mean(grad))

    gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
    if gmax > 10.0:
        grad *= (10.0 / gmax)

    return grad, score


def _softmax_vec(x):
    """Stable softmax from log-variables."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size == 0:
        return x
    x = x - float(np.max(x))
    x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 50.0)
    e = np.exp(x)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones_like(x, dtype=np.float64) / float(x.size)
    return e / s


def _adam_update(x, m, v, grad, step, lr):
    """Simple Adam-style update for log-positive variables."""
    grad = np.nan_to_num(grad, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    grad = grad - float(np.mean(grad))
    gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
    if gmax > 10.0:
        grad *= (10.0 / gmax)

    b1 = 0.90
    b2 = 0.999

    m = b1 * m + (1.0 - b1) * grad
    v = b2 * v + (1.0 - b2) * (grad * grad)

    bias1 = 1.0 - (0.90 ** int(step))
    bias2 = 1.0 - (0.999 ** int(step))
    mhat = m / bias1
    vhat = v / bias2

    x_new = x - float(lr) * (mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat) + 1e-8))
    x_new = x_new - float(np.mean(x_new))
    x_new = np.clip(x_new, -50.0, 50.0)
    return x_new, m, v


def make_filtered_c(c, n, rng):
    """
    Random low-order filtered version of the central-binomial sequence:
    a = c + sum r_k shift^k c, clipped if it remains nonnegative.
    """
    a = c.copy()
    for _ in range(int(rng.integers(1, 3))):
        if n <= 1:
            break
        p = int(rng.integers(1, n))
        if p < n:
            r = float(rng.uniform(-0.35, 0.70))
            a[p:] += r * c[:-p]

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return None
    if float(np.min(a)) < -1e-5:
        return None
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return _normalize(a)


def build_initial_candidates(c_raw, base_arr, N, L, rng, deadline):
    """Create and score many diverse initial sequences, returning top few."""
    c_norm = _normalize(c_raw)
    cands = [c_norm]

    if base_arr is not None and base_arr.size == N:
        bnorm = _normalize(base_arr)
        if float(bnorm.sum()) > 0.0:
            cands.append(bnorm)

    for sig in (0.05, 0.20, 0.60):
        a = c_raw * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sig, N))
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
        cands.append(_normalize(a))

    cands.append(_normalize(c_raw[::-1]))
    cands.append(_normalize(c_raw + c_raw[::-1]))

    alt = np.ones(N, dtype=np.float64)
    alt[1::2] = 0.5
    cands.append(_normalize(c_raw * alt))

    tilt = 1.0 + 0.5 * np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, N)
    cands.append(_normalize(c_raw * tilt))

    scored = []
    for a in cands:
        if a is None:
            continue
        s, _ = fft_score(a, L)
        if np.isfinite(s):
            scored.append((s, a))

    count = 0
    max_extra = 300
    while time.monotonic() < deadline and count < max_extra:
        a = make_filtered_c(c_raw, N, rng)
        if a is not None:
            s, _ = fft_score(a, L)
            if np.isfinite(s):
                scored.append((s, a))
        count += 1

    if not scored:
        return [c_norm], float("inf")

    scores = np.array([s for s, _ in scored], dtype=np.float64)
    order = np.argsort(scores)
    top = []
    for idx in order[:5]:
        top.append(scored[int(idx)][1])
    return top, float(scores[int(order[0])])


def reconstruct_from_b(q, n):
    """
    Given target first-n square coefficients q[0..n-1], recover a nonnegative
    sequence a if possible.  Uses recurrence:
        b_j = 2 a_0 a_j + sum_{i=1}^{j-1} a_i a_{j-i}.
    """
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    if q.size < n or q[0] <= 1e-300:
        return None

    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a0 = float(np.sqrt(q[0]))
    a[0] = a0
    bad = 0

    for j in range(1, n):
        if j > 1:
            r_val = float(np.dot(a[1:j], a[j - 1:0:-1]))
        else:
            r_val = 0.0

        val = float(q[j]) - r_val
        if val <= 1e-15:
            if val < -1e-8:
                bad += 1
                if bad > n // 20:
                    return None
            a[j] = 0.0
        else:
            aj = val / (2.0 * a0)
            if not np.isfinite(aj) or aj < -1e-9:
                return None
            a[j] = aj if aj > 0.0 else 0.0

    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    return a


def projection_perturb(a, L, rng, strength):
    """
    Use current b as a guide, nudge the first half toward a flatter target,
    then reconstruct a new sequence.  This provides larger structurally
    meaningful jumps than local coordinate moves.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    score, b = fft_score(a, L)
    if b is None:
        return None, float("inf")

    first = b[:n]
    M = float(np.max(first))
    if M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
        return None, float("inf")

    q = first.copy()
    mix = float(rng.uniform(0.20, 0.70))
    q = (1.0 - mix) * q + mix * M

    upper = M * (1.0 + 0.20 * float(strength))
    q = np.clip(q, 0.0, upper)

    noise_scale = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 0.20 * float(strength)))
    if noise_scale > 0.0:
        q += noise_scale * M * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)
        q = np.clip(q, 0.0, upper)

    new_a = reconstruct_from_b(q, n)
    if new_a is None:
        return None, float("inf")

    new_a = _normalize(new_a)
    if new_a is None:
        return None, float("inf")
    if float(new_a.sum()) <= 0.0:
        return None, float("inf")

    s, _ = fft_score(new_a, L)
    return new_a, s


def local_block_search(a, L, rng, deadline):
    """
    Final-stage local hill-climber using block multiplicative perturbations.
    """
    a = _normalize(a)
    n = a.size
    score, _ = fft_score(a, L)
    best = a.copy()
    best_score = score

    sigma = 0.25
    noimp = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if noimp > 400:
            a = best * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.25, n))
            a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
            a = _normalize(a)
            score, _ = fft_score(a, L)
            noimp = 0
            sigma = 0.5

        lo = max(1, n // 2000)
        hi = max(2, n // 20)
        if lo >= hi:
            hi = max(lo + 1, n // 20 + 1)
        width = int(rng.integers(lo, hi))
        width = min(width, n)
        start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))

        cand = a.copy()
        choice = float(rng.random())

        if choice < 0.45:
            delta = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, width))
        elif choice < 0.75:
            t = np.linspace(0.1, 0.9, width, endpoint=False)
            amp = float(rng.normal(0.0, sigma))
            delta = np.exp(amp * np.sin(np.pi * t) + rng.normal(0.0, 0.5 * sigma, width))
        else:
            delta = np.ones(width, dtype=np.float64)
            idx = int(rng.integers(0, width))
            if float(rng.random()) < 0.20:
                delta[idx] = 0.0
            else:
                delta[idx] = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.5))

        cand[start:start + width] *= delta
        cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
        cand = _normalize(cand)

        s, _ = fft_score(cand, L)
        if np.isfinite(s) and s < score:
            a = cand
            score = s
            if s < best_score:
                best = a.copy()
                best_score = s
            sigma = min(1.0, sigma * 1.05)
            noimp = 0
        else:
            sigma = max(0.02, sigma * 0.97)
            noimp += 1

    return best, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Multi-stage search for the best nonnegative height sequence.
    Returns a list of floats to be evaluated by the authoritative evaluator.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget_s = 2400.0
    budget_s = max(10.0, budget_s)
    total_deadline = start + budget_s - 5.0

    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) if seed is not None else 42)

    # Choose working length.  Use the provided construction when reasonable.
    base_arr = None
    base_len = 4806
    try:
        base_arr = _to_array(height_sequence_1)
    except Exception:
        base_arr = None

    if base_arr is not None and base_arr.size >= 64:
        cand_len = int(base_arr.size)
        if cand_len <= 10000:
            base_len = cand_len
        else:
            base_len = 10000
    if base_len < 512:
        base_len = 4806
    N = int(base_len)

    L = next_pow2(max(3 * N - 2, 16))
    c_raw = central_binom(N)
    c_norm = _normalize(c_raw)

    # Initial candidate construction, with a small time budget.
    init_deadline = start + min(60.0, max(10.0, budget_s * 0.02))
    top_a, init_score = build_initial_candidates(c_raw, base_arr, N, L, rng, init_deadline)
    if not top_a:
        top_a = [c_norm]
        init_score = float("inf")

    print("N =", N, "init_best =", init_score)

    # Validate a few initial points with the authoritative evaluator.
    best_internal_score = float("inf")
    best_internal_a = top_a[0]
    best_exact_score = float("inf")
    best_exact_seq = None
    last_exact = start

    for a in top_a[:4]:
        s, _ = fft_score(a, L)
        if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_internal_score:
            best_internal_score = s
            best_internal_a = a
        try:
            sc = evaluate_sequence(a.tolist())
        except Exception:
            sc = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact_score:
            best_exact_score = sc
            best_exact_seq = [float(x) for x in a]

    # Also make sure the central-binomial sequence is considered.
    sc = evaluate_sequence(c_norm.tolist())
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact_score:
        best_exact_score = sc
        best_exact_seq = [float(x) for x in c_norm]
    s, _ = fft_score(c_norm, L)
    if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_internal_score:
        best_internal_score = s
        best_internal_a = c_norm

    print("initial authoritative best =", best_exact_score)

    # Build a small population of log-variables states.
    K = min(4, len(top_a))
    if K == 0:
        K = 1
        top_a = [c_norm]

    states_x = []
    states_m = []
    states_v = []
    states_a = []
    states_score = []
    states_noimp = []

    for a in top_a[:K]:
        x = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-18))
        x = x - float(np.mean(x))
        x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 50.0)
        s, _ = fft_score(a, L)
        states_x.append(x)
        states_m.append(np.zeros(N, dtype=np.float64))
        states_v.append(np.zeros(N, dtype=np.float64))
        states_a.append(a)
        states_score.append(s if np.isfinite(s) else 1e9)
        states_noimp.append(0)

    local_time = min(300.0, max(60.0, budget_s * 0.12))
    opt_deadline = total_deadline - local_time - 3.0
    if opt_deadline < start + 5.0:
        opt_deadline = total_deadline - 3.0

    it = 0
    projected = 0
    max_proj = 8
    exact_interval = 45.0 if N <= 6000 else 90.0

    while time.monotonic() < opt_deadline:
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
        if elapsed < 200.0:
            beta = 8.0
        elif elapsed < 700.0:
            beta = 16.0
        elif elapsed < 1400.0:
            beta = 32.0
        else:
            beta = 64.0

        lr = 0.08
        si = it % K

        a = states_a[si]
        grad, cur_score = compute_grad(a, beta, L)
        if np.isfinite(cur_score) and cur_score < states_score[si]:
            states_score[si] = cur_score

        x_new, m_new, v_new = _adam_update(states_x[si], states_m[si], states_v[si], grad, it + 1, lr)
        cand = _softmax_vec(x_new)
        new_score, _ = fft_score(cand, L)

        improved = np.isfinite(new_score) and (((states_score[si] >= 1e9) and np.isfinite(new_score)) or (new_score < states_score[si]))
        accept = improved

        if not accept and np.isfinite(new_score) and np.isfinite(states_score[si]):
            rel = (new_score - states_score[si]) / max(1e-18, states_score[si])
            if rel < 0.04:
                if rng.random() < math.exp(-max(0.0, rel) / 0.012):
                    accept = True

        if accept:
            states_x[si] = x_new
            states_m[si] = m_new
            states_v[si] = v_new
            states_a[si] = cand
            states_score[si] = new_score if np.isfinite(new_score) else states_score[si]

            if improved:
                states_noimp[si] = 0
                if new_score < best_internal_score:
                    best_internal_score = new_score
                    best_internal_a = cand.copy()
                    if time.monotonic() - last_exact > exact_interval:
                        try:
                            sc = evaluate_sequence(cand.tolist())
                        except Exception:
                            sc = float("inf")
                        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact_score:
                            best_exact_score = sc
                            best_exact_seq = [float(x) for x in cand]
                        last_exact = time.monotonic()
            else:
                states_noimp[si] += 50
        else:
            states_noimp[si] += 1

        # If this state is stuck, inject a larger structural perturbation.
        if states_noimp[si] > 600:
            if projected < max_proj and time.monotonic() < opt_deadline - 120.0:
                new_a, new_s = projection_perturb(states_a[si], L, rng, float(rng.uniform(0.1, 0.8)))
                projected += 1
                if new_a is not None and np.isfinite(new_s) and new_n := 0:  # placeholder to avoid syntax; replaced below
                    pass
            else:
                if rng.random() < 0.5:
                    parent = int(np.argmin(states_score))
                    x2 = states_x[parent] + rng.normal(0.0, 0.15, N)
                else:
                    i1 = int(rng.integers(0, K))
                    i2 = int(rng.integers(0, K))
                    al = float(rng.uniform(0.2, 0.8))
                    x2 = al * states_x[i1] + (1.0 - al) * states_x[i2]
                    x2 += rng.normal(0.0, 0.10, N)
                x2 = x2 - float(np.mean(x2))
                x2 = np.clip(x2, -50.0, 50.0)
                a2 = _softmax_vec(x2)
                s2, _ = fft_score(a2, L)
                if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < states_score[si] + 0.10:
                    states_x[si] = x2
                    states_a[si] = a2
                    states_score[si] = s2
                    states_m[si] = np.zeros(N, dtype=np.float64)
                    states_v[si] = np.zeros(N, dtype=np.float64)
                    states_noimp[si] = 0

        it += 1
        if it % 2000 == 0:
            print(
                "iter", it,
                "elapsed", round(elapsed, 1), "s",
                "internal", f"{best_internal_score:.6f}",
                "authoritative", f"{best_exact_score:.6f}"
            )

    # Periodically/finally use the projection jump if we have not tried many.
    if projected < max_proj and time.monotonic() < total_deadline - 10.0:
        best_a_now = best_internal_a if best_internal_a is not None else top_a[0]
        new_a, new_s = projection_perturb(best_a_now, L, rng, 0.6)
        if new_a is not None and np.isfinite(new_s) and new_s < best_internal_score:
            best_internal_score = new_s
            best_internal_a = new_a

    # Local block polish for remaining time.
    if total_deadline - time.monotonic() > 20.0:
        start_a = best_internal_a if best_internal_a is not None else top_a[0]
        local_a, local_score = local_block_search(start_a, L, rng, total_deadline - 3.0)
        if local_a is not None and np.isfinite(local_score) and local_score < best_internal_score:
            best_internal_score = local_score
            best_internal_a = local_a
        try:
            sc = evaluate_sequence(local_a.tolist())
        except Exception:
            sc = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact_score:
            best_exact_score = sc
            best_exact_seq = [float(x) for x in local_a]

    # Final validation and return best exact sequence found.
    final_candidates = []
    if best_exact_seq is not None:
        final_candidates.append(_to_array(best_exact_seq))
    if best_internal_a is not None:
        final_candidates.append(_to_array(best_internal_a))
    for a in top_a[:2]:
        final_candidates.append(_to_array(a))
    final_candidates.append(c_norm)

    best_final_score = float("inf")
    best_final_arr = None

    for arr in final_candidates:
        if arr is None:
            continue
        arr = _to_array(arr)
        if arr is None:
            continue
        arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if float(arr.sum()) <= 0.01:
            arr = _normalize(arr)
            if float(arr.sum()) <= 0.01:
                continue
        try:
            sc = evaluate_sequence(arr.tolist())
        except Exception:
            sc = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_final_score:
            best_final_score = sc
            best_final_arr = arr

    if best_final_arr is None:
        best_final_arr = c_norm
        try:
            best_final_score = float(evaluate_sequence(c_norm.tolist()))
        except Exception:
            best_final_score = float("inf")

    print("final authoritative score =", best_final_score)
    return [float(x) for x in best_final_arr]
```
